#include <chrono>
#include <cmath>
#include <cstdio>
#include <cstdlib>
#include <cstring>
#include <vector>

#include "../common/results_output.hpp"

using index_t = uint32_t;

// Constants
constexpr double MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR = 0.02;

// ****************************************************************************
// Function: fill
//
// Purpose:
//   Initialize array with random values
//
// Arguments:
//   A: pointer to the array to initialize
//   n: number of elements in the array
//   maxVal: specifies range of random values [0, maxVal]
//
// ****************************************************************************
void fill(double* A, const index_t n, const double maxVal) {
    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        A[i] = maxVal * (rand() / (static_cast<double>(RAND_MAX) + 1.0));
    }
}

// ****************************************************************************
// Function: initRandomMatrix
//
// Purpose:
//   Assigns random positions to a given number of elements in a square
//   matrix. The function encodes these positions in compressed sparse
//   row (CSR) format.
//
// Arguments:
//   cols:          array for column indexes of elements (size should be = n)
//   rowDelimiters: array of size dim+1 holding indices to rows;
//                  last element is the index one past the last element
//   n:             number of nonzero elements
//   dim:           number of rows/columns in the matrix
//
// ****************************************************************************
void initRandomMatrix(index_t* cols, index_t* rowDelimiters, const index_t n, const index_t dim) {
    index_t nnzAssigned = 0;

    // Figure out the probability that a nonzero should be assigned
    double prob = static_cast<double>(n) / (static_cast<double>(dim) * static_cast<double>(dim));

    // Seed random number generator
    srand(8675309);

    // Randomly decide whether entry i,j gets a value, but ensure n values are assigned
    bool fillRemaining = false;
    for (index_t i = 0; i < dim; ++i) {
        rowDelimiters[i] = nnzAssigned;
        for (index_t j = 0; j < dim; ++j) {
            index_t numEntriesLeft = (dim * dim) - ((i * dim) + j);
            index_t needToAssign = n - nnzAssigned;
            if (numEntriesLeft <= needToAssign) {
                fillRemaining = true;
            }
            double randVal = static_cast<double>(rand()) / RAND_MAX;
            if ((nnzAssigned < n && randVal <= prob) || fillRemaining) {
                // Assign (i,j) a value
                cols[nnzAssigned] = j;
                nnzAssigned++;
            }
        }
    }
    // Convention: put the number of non-zeroes at the end of the row delimiters array
    rowDelimiters[dim] = n;
}

// ****************************************************************************
// Function: spmvCpu
//
// Purpose:
//   Computes sparse matrix-vector multiplication using CSR format
//
// Arguments:
//   val: array holding the non-zero values for the matrix
//   cols: array of column indices for each element
//   rowDelimiters: array of size dim+1 holding indices to rows;
//                  last element is the index one past the last element
//   vec: dense vector of size dim to be used for multiplication
//   dim: number of rows/columns in the matrix
//   out: output - result from the spmv calculation
//
// ****************************************************************************
void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* cols, const index_t* rowDelimiters,
             const double* vec, const index_t dim, double* out) {
    for (index_t i = 0; i < dim; ++i) {
        double t = 0.0;
        for (index_t j = rowDelimiters[i]; j < rowDelimiters[i + 1]; ++j) {
            const auto col = cols[j];
            t += val[j] * vec[col];
        }
        out[i] = t;
    }
}

// ****************************************************************************
// Function: verifyResults
//
// Purpose:
//   Verifies correctness of results by comparing to reference solution
//
// Arguments:
//   reference: array holding the reference result vector
//   result: array holding the result vector to verify
//   size: number of elements per vector
//
// ****************************************************************************
bool verifyResults(const double* reference, const double* result, const index_t size) {
    for (index_t i = 0; i < size; ++i) {
        const double ref = reference[i];
        const double res = result[i];
        if (std::abs(ref) < 1e-10) {
            // For very small values, check absolute error
            if (std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {
                printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n", i, ref, res);
                return false;
            }
        } else {
            // Check relative error
            const double relError = std::abs((res - ref) / ref);
            if (relError > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {
                printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e (rel error: %.10e)\n",
                       i, ref, res, relError);
                return false;
            }
        }
    }
    return true;
}

void printUsage(const char* progName) {
    printf("Usage: %s [options]\n", progName);
    printf("Options:\n");
    printf("  -n <num>     Number of rows/columns in the matrix (default: 1024)\n");
    printf("  -s <num>     Sparsity: 1 out of N entries is non-zero (default: 10)\n");
    printf("  -i <num>     Number of iterations (default: 10)\n");
    printf("  -m <val>     Maximum value for matrix and vector elements (default: 1.0)\n");
    printf("  -v           Enable validation\n");
    printf("  -r           Print results for external validation\n");
    printf("  -h           Show this help message\n");
}

int main(int argc, char** argv) {
    index_t numRows = 1024;
    index_t sparsity = 10;
    index_t iterations = 10;
    double maxVal = 1.0;
    bool validate = false;
    bool printResults = false;

    // Parse command line arguments
    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {
        if (strcmp(argv[i], "-n") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {
            numRows = static_cast<index_t>(atoi(argv[++i]));
        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], "-s") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {
            sparsity = static_cast<index_t>(atoi(argv[++i]));
        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], "-i") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {
            iterations = static_cast<index_t>(atoi(argv[++i]));
        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], "-m") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {
            maxVal = atof(argv[++i]);
        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], "-v") == 0) {
            validate = true;
        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], "-r") == 0) {
            printResults = true;
        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], "-h") == 0) {
            printUsage(argv[0]);
            return 0;
        } else {
            printf("Unknown option: %s\n", argv[i]);
            printUsage(argv[0]);
            return 1;
        }
    }

    // Calculate number of non-zero elements
    const index_t nItems = (numRows * numRows) / sparsity;

    printf("Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication (SpMV) Benchmark\n");
    printf("Matrix size: %u x %u\n", numRows, numRows);
    printf("Sparsity: 1 out of %u entries is non-zero\n", sparsity);
    printf("Non-zero elements: %u (%.2f%% sparse)\n", 
           nItems, 100.0 * (1.0 - static_cast<double>(nItems) / (numRows * numRows)));
    printf("Iterations: %u\n", iterations);
    printf("Max value: %.2f\n", maxVal);
    printf("Validation: %s\n", validate ? "enabled" : "disabled");

    // Allocate and initialize data structures
    std::vector<double> h_val(nItems);                  // Non-zero values
    std::vector<index_t> h_cols(nItems);                // Column indices
    std::vector<index_t> h_rowDelimiters(numRows + 1);  // Row delimiters
    std::vector<double> h_vec(numRows);                 // Dense vector
    std::vector<double> h_out(numRows);                 // Output vector

    printf("Initializing data structures...\n");
    fill(h_vec.data(), numRows, maxVal);
    fill(h_val.data(), nItems, maxVal);
    initRandomMatrix(h_cols.data(), h_rowDelimiters.data(), nItems, numRows);

    // For validation, compute reference solution
    std::vector<double> h_reference;
    if (validate) {
        printf("Computing reference solution...\n");
        h_reference.resize(numRows);
        spmvCpu(h_val.data(), h_cols.data(), h_rowDelimiters.data(), 
                h_vec.data(), numRows, h_reference.data());
    }

    // Perform SpMV computation
    printf("Computing SpMV...\n");
    auto start = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();

    for (index_t iter = 0; iter < iterations; ++iter) {
        spmvCpu(h_val.data(), h_cols.data(), h_rowDelimiters.data(),
                h_vec.data(), numRows, h_out.data());
    }

    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();
    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);

    printf("Computation time: %ld ms\n", duration.count());
    
    // Calculate performance metrics
    const double gflops = (2.0 * nItems * iterations) / (duration.count() / 1000.0) / 1e9;
    const double avgTime = duration.count() / static_cast<double>(iterations);
    
    printf("Average time per iteration: %.3f ms\n", avgTime);
    printf("Performance: %.3f GFLOPS\n", gflops);
    
    // Print results for external validation
    if (printResults) {
        print_results(h_out, "OutputVector");
    }

    // Validation
    if (validate) {
        printf("Validating result...\n");
        const bool valid = verifyResults(h_reference.data(), h_out.data(), numRows);

        if (valid) {
            printf("Validation: PASSED\n");
            return 0;
        } else {
            printf("Validation: FAILED\n");
            return 1;
        }
    }

    return 0;

#pragma once

#include <algorithm>
#include <cinttypes>
#include <cmath>
#include <cstdio>
#include <vector>

#include "hash.hpp"

// Kahan summation for numerically stable sum
template <typename T>
T kahan_sum(const std::vector<T>& data) {
    T sum = 0.0;
    T c = 0.0;
    for (const auto& val : data) {
        T y = val - c;
        T t = sum + y;
        c = (t - sum) - y;
        sum = t;
    }
    return sum;
}

// Print results for floating-point arrays
template <typename T>
void print_results(const std::vector<T>& data, const char* name = "Result") {
    if (data.empty()) {
        printf("=== RESULTS ===\n");
        printf("Name: %s\n", name);
        printf("Elements: 0\n");
        printf("=== END RESULTS ===\n");
        return;
    }

    const size_t N = data.size();
    
    // Compute statistics
    T sum = kahan_sum(data);
    T min_val = data[0];
    T max_val = data[0];
    for (const auto& val : data) {
        min_val = std::min(min_val, val);
        max_val = std::max(max_val, val);
    }
    
    // Compute hash
    hash h;
    for (const auto& val : data) {
        h.add(val);
    }
    
    // Sample indices: 0, N/4, N/2, 3N/4, N-1
    size_t indices[5] = {0, N/4, N/2, 3*N/4, N-1};
    
    printf("=== RESULTS ===\n");
    printf("Name: %s\n", name);
    printf("Elements: %zu\n", N);
    printf("Sum: %.17e\n", static_cast<double>(sum));
    printf("Min: %.17e\n", static_cast<double>(min_val));
    printf("Max: %.17e\n", static_cast<double>(max_val));
    for (int i = 0; i < 5; ++i) {
        printf("Sample[%zu]: %.17e\n", indices[i], static_cast<double>(data[indices[i]]));
    }
    printf("Hash: %016" PRIx64 "\n", h.get());
    printf("=== END RESULTS ===\n");
}

// Print results for integer arrays (uses exact hash comparison)
template <typename T>
void print_results_int(const std::vector<T>& data, const char* name = "Result") {
    if (data.empty()) {
        printf("=== RESULTS ===\n");
        printf("Name: %s\n", name);
        printf("Elements: 0\n");
        printf("=== END RESULTS ===\n");
        return;
    }

    const size_t N = data.size();
    
    // Compute hash
    hash h;
    for (const auto& val : data) {
        h.add(val);
    }
    
    // Compute min/max
    T min_val = data[0];
    T max_val = data[0];
    for (const auto& val : data) {
        min_val = std::min(min_val, val);
        max_val = std::max(max_val, val);
    }
    
    // Sample indices: 0, N/4, N/2, 3N/4, N-1
    size_t indices[5] = {0, N/4, N/2, 3*N/4, N-1};
    
    printf("=== RESULTS ===\n");
    printf("Name: %s\n", name);
    printf("Elements: %zu\n", N);
    printf("Min: %lld\n", static_cast<long long>(min_val));
    printf("Max: %lld\n", static_cast<long long>(max_val));
    for (int i = 0; i < 5; ++i) {
        printf("Sample[%zu]: %lld\n", indices[i], static_cast<long long>(data[indices[i]]));
    }
    printf("Hash: %016" PRIx64 "\n", h.get());
    printf("=== END RESULTS ===\n");
}

-// Function: verifyResults
-//

-//   Verifies correctness of results by comparing to reference solution

+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
+    }
+}
+

-// ****************************************************************************
-// Function: verifyResults
-//
-// Purpose:
-//   Verifies correctness of results by comparing to reference solution
-//
-// Arguments:
-//   reference: array holding the reference result vector
-//   result: array holding the result vector to verify
-//   size: number of elements per vector
-//
-// ****************************************************************************
+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
+    }
+}
+

         const double ref = reference[i];

-        if (std::abs(ref) < 1e-10) {
-            // For very small values, check absolute error
-            if (std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {
-                printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e\n", i, ref, res);
-                return false;
-            }
-        } else {
-            // Check relative error
-            const double relError = std::abs((res - ref) / ref);
-            if (relError > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {
-                printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e (rel error: %.10e)\n",
-                       i, ref, res, relError);
-                return false;
-            }
+        const bool bad = std::abs(ref) < 1e-10
+                             ? std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR
+                             : std::abs((res - ref) / ref) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR;
+        if (bad) {
+            firstBad = i;
         }
     }
-    return true;
+    if (firstBad == static_cast<int64_t>(size)) {
+        return true;
+    }
+
+    const double ref = reference[firstBad];
+    const double res = result[firstBad];
+    const double rel = std::abs(ref) < 1e-10 ? std::abs(res) : std::abs((res - ref) / ref);
+    std::printf("Validation failed at index %lld: reference %.10e, got %.10e "
+                "(error: %.10e)\n", static_cast<long long>(firstBad), ref, res, rel);

 }

codex
The hybrid implementation is now in place: CSR rows are distributed once with MPI, each rank computes its partition on its locally assigned GPU, and OpenMP handles host-parallel CSR preprocessing and validation. I’ve also kept initialization and output gathering outside the timed region so reported performance isolates repeated SpMV work. Next I’m compiling and running single- and multi-rank validation.

+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
+    }
+}

+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
+    }
+}
+

-// ****************************************************************************

-//   Verifies correctness of results by comparing to reference solution

-//   reference: array holding the reference result vector
-//   result: array holding the result vector to verify

-// ****************************************************************************
+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
+    }

                          omit the preimage for deletes
    -C, --find-copies[=<n>]
                          detect copies
    --[no-]find-copies-harder
                          use unmodified files as source to find copies
    --no-renames          disable rename detection
    --[no-]rename-empty   use empty blobs as rename source
    --[no-]follow         continue listing the history of a file beyond renames
    -l <n>                prevent rename/copy detection if the number of rename/copy targets exceeds given limit

Diff algorithm options
    --minimal             produce the smallest possible diff
    -w, --ignore-all-space
                          ignore whitespace when comparing lines
    -b, --ignore-space-change
                          ignore changes in amount of whitespace
    --ignore-space-at-eol ignore changes in whitespace at EOL
    --ignore-cr-at-eol    ignore carrier-return at the end of line
    --ignore-blank-lines  ignore changes whose lines are all blank
    -I, --[no-]ignore-matching-lines <regex>
                          ignore changes whose all lines match <regex>
    --[no-]indent-heuristic
                          heuristic to shift diff hunk boundaries for easy reading
    --patience            generate diff using the "patience diff" algorithm
    --histogram           generate diff using the "histogram diff" algorithm
    --diff-algorithm <algorithm>
                          choose a diff algorithm
    --anchored <text>     generate diff using the "anchored diff" algorithm
    --word-diff[=<mode>]  show word diff, using <mode> to delimit changed words
    --word-diff-regex <regex>
                          use <regex> to decide what a word is
    --color-words[=<regex>]
                          equivalent to --word-diff=color --word-diff-regex=<regex>
    --[no-]color-moved[=<mode>]
                          moved lines of code are colored differently
    --[no-]color-moved-ws <mode>
                          how white spaces are ignored in --color-moved

Other diff options
    --[no-]relative[=<prefix>]
                          when run from subdir, exclude changes outside and show relative paths
    -a, --[no-]text       treat all files as text
    -R                    swap two inputs, reverse the diff
    --[no-]exit-code      exit with 1 if there were differences, 0 otherwise
    --[no-]quiet          disable all output of the program
    --[no-]ext-diff       allow an external diff helper to be executed
    --[no-]textconv       run external text conversion filters when comparing binary files
    --ignore-submodules[=<when>]
                          ignore changes to submodules in the diff generation
    --submodule[=<format>]
                          specify how differences in submodules are shown
    --ita-invisible-in-index
                          hide 'git add -N' entries from the index
    --ita-visible-in-index
                          treat 'git add -N' entries as real in the index
    -S <string>           look for differences that change the number of occurrences of the specified string
    -G <regex>            look for differences that change the number of occurrences of the specified regex
    --pickaxe-all         show all changes in the changeset with -S or -G
    --pickaxe-regex       treat <string> in -S as extended POSIX regular expression
    -O <file>             control the order in which files appear in the output
    --rotate-to <path>    show the change in the specified path first
    --skip-to <path>      skip the output to the specified path
    --find-object <object-id>
                          look for differences that change the number of occurrences of the specified object
    --diff-filter [(A|C|D|M|R|T|U|X|B)...[*]]
                          select files by diff type
    --output <file>       output to a specific file

+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
+    }
+}
+

+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
+    }
+}
+

-// ****************************************************************************
+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
+    }
+}
+

+static void cudaCheck(cudaError_t status, const char* operation, MPI_Comm comm) {
+    if (status != cudaSuccess) {
+        int rank = -1;
+        MPI_Comm_rank(comm, &rank);
+        std::fprintf(stderr, "Rank %d: CUDA error in %s: %s\n", rank, operation,

 void fill(double* A, const index_t n, const double maxVal) {
     for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
         A[i] = maxVal * (rand() / (static_cast<double>(RAND_MAX) + 1.0));
     }

-// ****************************************************************************
-// Function: verifyResults

-// Purpose:
-//   Verifies correctness of results by comparing to reference solution

-// ****************************************************************************
+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
+    }
+}
+

-        if (std::abs(ref) < 1e-10) {
-            // For very small values, check absolute error
-            if (std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {

-                return false;
-            }
-        } else {
-            // Check relative error
-            const double relError = std::abs((res - ref) / ref);
-            if (relError > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {
-                printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e (rel error: %.10e)\n",
-                       i, ref, res, relError);
-                return false;
-            }
+        const bool bad = std::abs(ref) < 1e-10
+                             ? std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR
+                             : std::abs((res - ref) / ref) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR;
+        if (bad) {
+            firstBad = i;
         }
     }
-    return true;
+    if (firstBad == static_cast<int64_t>(size)) {
+        return true;
+    }
+
+    const double ref = reference[firstBad];
+    const double res = result[firstBad];
+    const double rel = std::abs(ref) < 1e-10 ? std::abs(res) : std::abs((res - ref) / ref);
+    std::printf("Validation failed at index %lld: reference %.10e, got %.10e "
+                "(error: %.10e)\n", static_cast<long long>(firstBad), ref, res, rel);

+            if (verifyResults(reference.data(), hOut.data(), numRows)) {
+                std::printf("Validation: PASSED\n");

+            }
         }
     }

+    MPI_Bcast(&exitCode, 1, MPI_INT, 0, MPI_COMM_WORLD);
+    MPI_Finalize();

+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
+    }
+}

+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
+    }
+}

+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
+    }
+}
+

-        if (std::abs(ref) < 1e-10) {

-            if (std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {

-                return false;
-            }
-        } else {
-            // Check relative error
-            const double relError = std::abs((res - ref) / ref);
-            if (relError > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR) {
-                printf("Validation failed at index %u: reference %.10e, got %.10e (rel error: %.10e)\n",
-                       i, ref, res, relError);
-                return false;

+        const bool bad = std::abs(ref) < 1e-10
+                             ? std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR
+                             : std::abs((res - ref) / ref) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR;

+    if (firstBad == static_cast<int64_t>(size)) {

+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)
+    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(dim); ++i) {
+        double sum = 0.0;
+        for (index_t j = rows[i]; j < rows[i + 1]; ++j) {
+            sum += val[j] * vec[cols[j]];
         }
-        out[i] = t;
+        out[i] = sum;
+    }
+}
+
+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
     }
 }

+void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* cols, const index_t* rows,
              const double* vec, const index_t dim, double* out) {

+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)

-        out[i] = t;

+}
+
+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
     }
 }

+        spmvCsrWarpKernel<<<blocks, CUDA_BLOCK_SIZE>>>(dVal, dCols, dRows, dVec, localRows, dOut);

+    std::vector<double> localOut(needOutput ? localRows : 0);

+        cudaCheck(cudaMemcpy(localOut.data(), dOut, localRows * sizeof(double),
+                             cudaMemcpyDeviceToHost), "copy output", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
+    }
+    if (needOutput) {

codex
The edge-case run with four ranks and only two matrix rows also passed, so zero-row MPI ranks are handled correctly. I found and corrected one build-performance issue as well: CMake had been retaining nvcc’s legacy `sm_52` default; fresh builds now generate native GPU code unless the cluster architecture list is explicitly supplied. I also release host staging buffers after GPU upload to keep per-rank memory scalable.

+
+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
     }
 }

+
+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
     }
 }

    22	static void cudaCheck(cudaError_t status, const char* operation, MPI_Comm comm) {
    23	    if (status != cudaSuccess) {
    24	        int rank = -1;
    25	        MPI_Comm_rank(comm, &rank);
    26	        std::fprintf(stderr, "Rank %d: CUDA error in %s: %s\n", rank, operation,
    27	                     cudaGetErrorString(status));
    28	        MPI_Abort(comm, EXIT_FAILURE);
    29	    }
    30	}
    31	
    32	void fill(double* A, const index_t n, const double maxVal) {
    33	    for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    34	        A[i] = maxVal * (rand() / (static_cast<double>(RAND_MAX) + 1.0));
    35	    }
    36	}
    37	
    38	void initRandomMatrix(index_t* cols, index_t* rowDelimiters, const index_t n,
    39	                      const index_t dim) {
    40	    index_t nnzAssigned = 0;
    41	    const double prob = static_cast<double>(n) /
    42	                        (static_cast<double>(dim) * static_cast<double>(dim));
    43	    srand(8675309);
    44	
    45	    bool fillRemaining = false;
    46	    for (index_t i = 0; i < dim; ++i) {
    47	        rowDelimiters[i] = nnzAssigned;
    48	        for (index_t j = 0; j < dim; ++j) {
    49	            const uint64_t linear = static_cast<uint64_t>(i) * dim + j;
    50	            const uint64_t entriesLeft = static_cast<uint64_t>(dim) * dim - linear;
    51	            const index_t needToAssign = n - nnzAssigned;
    52	            if (entriesLeft <= needToAssign) {
    53	                fillRemaining = true;
    54	            }
    55	            const double randVal = static_cast<double>(rand()) / RAND_MAX;
    56	            if ((nnzAssigned < n && randVal <= prob) || fillRemaining) {
    57	                cols[nnzAssigned++] = j;
    58	            }
    59	        }
    60	    }
    61	    rowDelimiters[dim] = n;
    62	}
    63	
    64	void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* cols, const index_t* rows,
    65	             const double* vec, const index_t dim, double* out) {
    66	#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)
    67	    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(dim); ++i) {
    68	        double sum = 0.0;
    69	        for (index_t j = rows[i]; j < rows[i + 1]; ++j) {
    70	            sum += val[j] * vec[cols[j]];
    71	        }
    72	        out[i] = sum;
    73	    }
    74	}
    75	
    76	// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
    77	// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
    78	// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
    79	__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
    80	                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
    81	                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
    82	                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
    83	                                  index_t localRows,
    84	                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
    85	    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    86	    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
    87	    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
    88	    if (row >= localRows) {
    89	        return;
    90	    }
    91	
    92	    double sum = 0.0;
    93	    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
    94	        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
    95	    }
    96	    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
    97	        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
    98	    }
    99	    if (lane == 0) {
   100	        out[row] = sum;
   101	    }
   102	}
   103	
   104	bool verifyResults(const double* reference, const double* result, const index_t size) {
   105	    int64_t firstBad = static_cast<int64_t>(size);
   106	#pragma omp parallel for reduction(min : firstBad) schedule(static)
   107	    for (int64_t i = 0; i < static_cast<int64_t>(size); ++i) {
   108	        const double ref = reference[i];
   109	        const double res = result[i];
   110	        const bool bad = std::abs(ref) < 1e-10
   111	                             ? std::abs(res) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR
   112	                             : std::abs((res - ref) / ref) > MAX_RELATIVE_ERROR;
   113	        if (bad) {
   114	            firstBad = i;
   115	        }
   116	    }
   117	    if (firstBad == static_cast<int64_t>(size)) {
   118	        return true;
   119	    }
   120	
   121	    const double ref = reference[firstBad];
   122	    const double res = result[firstBad];
   123	    const double rel = std::abs(ref) < 1e-10 ? std::abs(res) : std::abs((res - ref) / ref);
   124	    std::printf("Validation failed at index %lld: reference %.10e, got %.10e "
   125	                "(error: %.10e)\n", static_cast<long long>(firstBad), ref, res, rel);
   126	    return false;
   127	}
   128	
   129	void printUsage(const char* progName) {
   130	    std::printf("Usage: %s [options]\n", progName);
   131	    std::printf("Options:\n");
   132	    std::printf("  -n <num>     Number of rows/columns in the matrix (default: 1024)\n");
   133	    std::printf("  -s <num>     Sparsity: 1 out of N entries is non-zero (default: 10)\n");
   134	    std::printf("  -i <num>     Number of iterations (default: 10)\n");
   135	    std::printf("  -m <val>     Maximum element value (default: 1.0)\n");
   136	    std::printf("  -v           Enable validation\n");
   137	    std::printf("  -r           Print results for external validation\n");
   138	    std::printf("  -h           Show this help message\n");
   139	}
   140	
   141	int main(int argc, char** argv) {
   142	    int provided = 0;
   143	    MPI_Init_thread(&argc, &argv, MPI_THREAD_FUNNELED, &provided);
   144	    int rank = 0;
   145	    int worldSize = 1;
   146	    MPI_Comm_rank(MPI_COMM_WORLD, &rank);
   147	    MPI_Comm_size(MPI_COMM_WORLD, &worldSize);
   148	    if (provided < MPI_THREAD_FUNNELED) {
   149	        if (rank == 0) std::fprintf(stderr, "MPI does not provide required thread support\n");
   150	        MPI_Abort(MPI_COMM_WORLD, EXIT_FAILURE);
   151	    }
   152	
   153	    index_t numRows = 1024;
   154	    index_t sparsity = 10;
   155	    index_t iterations = 10;
   156	    double maxVal = 1.0;
   157	    bool validate = false;
   158	    bool printResults = false;
   159	    int parseStatus = 0;
   160	
   161	    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {
   162	        if (std::strcmp(argv[i], "-n") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {
   163	            numRows = static_cast<index_t>(std::strtoul(argv[++i], nullptr, 10));
   164	        } else if (std::strcmp(argv[i], "-s") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {
   165	            sparsity = static_cast<index_t>(std::strtoul(argv[++i], nullptr, 10));
   166	        } else if (std::strcmp(argv[i], "-i") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {
   167	            iterations = static_cast<index_t>(std::strtoul(argv[++i], nullptr, 10));
   168	        } else if (std::strcmp(argv[i], "-m") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {
   169	            maxVal = std::strtod(argv[++i], nullptr);
   170	        } else if (std::strcmp(argv[i], "-v") == 0) {
   171	            validate = true;
   172	        } else if (std::strcmp(argv[i], "-r") == 0) {
   173	            printResults = true;
   174	        } else if (std::strcmp(argv[i], "-h") == 0) {
   175	            if (rank == 0) printUsage(argv[0]);
   176	            MPI_Finalize();
   177	            return 0;
   178	        } else {
   179	            if (rank == 0) {
   180	                std::printf("Unknown or incomplete option: %s\n", argv[i]);
   180	                std::printf("Unknown or incomplete option: %s\n", argv[i]);
   181	                printUsage(argv[0]);
   182	            }
   183	            parseStatus = 1;
   184	            break;
   185	        }
   186	    }
   187	
   188	    const uint64_t matrixEntries = static_cast<uint64_t>(numRows) * numRows;
   189	    const uint64_t nnz64 = sparsity == 0 ? 0 : matrixEntries / sparsity;
   190	    if (parseStatus || numRows == 0 || sparsity == 0 || iterations == 0 ||
   191	        nnz64 > std::numeric_limits<index_t>::max() ||
   192	        numRows > static_cast<index_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max())) {
   193	        if (rank == 0 && !parseStatus) {
   194	            std::fprintf(stderr, "Invalid size/options: dimensions, sparsity, and iterations must "
   195	                                 "be positive and the CSR/MPI counts must fit in 32 bits\n");
   196	        }
   197	        MPI_Finalize();
   198	        return EXIT_FAILURE;
   199	    }
   200	    const index_t nItems = static_cast<index_t>(nnz64);
   201	
   202	    // Balanced contiguous row ownership. Contiguity minimizes metadata and
   203	    // enables direct Gatherv of the final output.
   204	    std::vector<int> rowCounts(worldSize), rowDispls(worldSize);
   205	    for (int r = 0; r < worldSize; ++r) {
   206	        const uint64_t begin = static_cast<uint64_t>(numRows) * r / worldSize;
   207	        const uint64_t end = static_cast<uint64_t>(numRows) * (r + 1) / worldSize;
   208	        rowDispls[r] = static_cast<int>(begin);
   209	        rowCounts[r] = static_cast<int>(end - begin);
   210	    }
   211	    const index_t localRows = static_cast<index_t>(rowCounts[rank]);
   212	
   213	    std::vector<double> hVal;
   214	    std::vector<index_t> hCols;
   215	    std::vector<index_t> hRows;
   216	    std::vector<double> hVec(numRows);
   217	    std::vector<double> hOut;
   218	    if (rank == 0) {
   219	        std::printf("Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication (SpMV) Benchmark\n");
   220	        std::printf("Matrix size: %u x %u\n", numRows, numRows);
   221	        std::printf("Sparsity: 1 out of %u entries is non-zero\n", sparsity);
   222	        std::printf("Non-zero elements: %u (%.2f%% sparse)\n", nItems,
   223	                    100.0 * (1.0 - static_cast<double>(nItems) / matrixEntries));
   224	        std::printf("Iterations: %u\n", iterations);
   225	        std::printf("Max value: %.2f\n", maxVal);
   226	        std::printf("MPI ranks: %d, OpenMP threads/rank: %d\n", worldSize,
   227	                    omp_get_max_threads());
   228	        std::printf("Validation: %s\n", validate ? "enabled" : "disabled");
   229	        std::printf("Initializing and distributing data structures...\n");
   230	
   231	        hVal.resize(nItems);
   232	        hCols.resize(nItems);
   233	        hRows.resize(static_cast<size_t>(numRows) + 1);
   234	        hOut.resize(numRows);
   235	        fill(hVec.data(), numRows, maxVal);
   236	        fill(hVal.data(), nItems, maxVal);
   237	        initRandomMatrix(hCols.data(), hRows.data(), nItems, numRows);
   238	    }
   239	
   240	    std::vector<int> nnzCounts(worldSize), nnzDispls(worldSize), rowMetaCounts(worldSize);
   241	    int distributionValid = 1;
   242	    if (rank == 0) {
   243	        for (int r = 0; r < worldSize; ++r) {
   244	            const index_t begin = hRows[rowDispls[r]];
   245	            const index_t end = hRows[rowDispls[r] + rowCounts[r]];
   246	            if (end - begin > static_cast<index_t>(std::numeric_limits<int>::max())) {
   247	                distributionValid = 0;
   248	            }
   249	            nnzDispls[r] = static_cast<int>(begin);
   250	            nnzCounts[r] = static_cast<int>(end - begin);
   251	            rowMetaCounts[r] = rowCounts[r] + 1;
   252	        }
   253	    }
   254	    MPI_Bcast(&distributionValid, 1, MPI_INT, 0, MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   255	    if (!distributionValid) {
   256	        if (rank == 0) std::fprintf(stderr, "A rank's CSR partition exceeds MPI count limits\n");
   257	        MPI_Finalize();
   258	        return EXIT_FAILURE;
   259	    }
   260	
   261	    int localNnzInt = 0;
   262	    MPI_Scatter(nnzCounts.data(), 1, MPI_INT, &localNnzInt, 1, MPI_INT, 0,
   263	                MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   264	    const index_t localNnz = static_cast<index_t>(localNnzInt);
   265	    std::vector<double> localVal(localNnz);
   266	    std::vector<index_t> localCols(localNnz);
   267	    std::vector<index_t> localRowOffsets(static_cast<size_t>(localRows) + 1);
   268	
   269	    MPI_Bcast(hVec.data(), static_cast<int>(numRows), MPI_DOUBLE, 0, MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   270	    MPI_Scatterv(rank == 0 ? hVal.data() : nullptr, nnzCounts.data(), nnzDispls.data(),
   271	                 MPI_DOUBLE, localVal.data(), localNnzInt, MPI_DOUBLE, 0, MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   272	    MPI_Scatterv(rank == 0 ? hCols.data() : nullptr, nnzCounts.data(), nnzDispls.data(),
   273	                 MPI_UINT32_T, localCols.data(), localNnzInt, MPI_UINT32_T, 0,
   274	                 MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   275	    MPI_Scatterv(rank == 0 ? hRows.data() : nullptr, rowMetaCounts.data(),
   276	                 rowDispls.data(), MPI_UINT32_T, localRowOffsets.data(),
   277	                 static_cast<int>(localRows + 1), MPI_UINT32_T, 0, MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   278	
   279	    const index_t partitionBase = localRowOffsets[0];
   280	#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)
   281	    for (int64_t i = 0; i <= static_cast<int64_t>(localRows); ++i) {
   282	        localRowOffsets[i] -= partitionBase;
   283	    }
   284	
   285	    // Map ranks on the same host round-robin onto its visible accelerators.
   286	    MPI_Comm localComm;
   287	    MPI_Comm_split_type(MPI_COMM_WORLD, MPI_COMM_TYPE_SHARED, rank, MPI_INFO_NULL, &localComm);
   288	    int localRank = 0;
   289	    MPI_Comm_rank(localComm, &localRank);
   290	    int deviceCount = 0;
   291	    cudaCheck(cudaGetDeviceCount(&deviceCount), "cudaGetDeviceCount", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   292	    if (deviceCount == 0) {
   293	        if (rank == 0) std::fprintf(stderr, "No CUDA devices are visible\n");
   294	        MPI_Abort(MPI_COMM_WORLD, EXIT_FAILURE);
   295	    }
   296	    const int device = localRank % deviceCount;
   297	    cudaCheck(cudaSetDevice(device), "cudaSetDevice", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   298	    cudaCheck(cudaFree(nullptr), "CUDA context initialization", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   299	    MPI_Comm_free(&localComm);
   300	
   301	    double *dVal = nullptr, *dVec = nullptr, *dOut = nullptr;
   302	    index_t *dCols = nullptr, *dRows = nullptr;
   303	    cudaCheck(cudaMalloc(&dVal, std::max<size_t>(1, localNnz) * sizeof(double)),
   304	              "cudaMalloc(values)", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   305	    cudaCheck(cudaMalloc(&dCols, std::max<size_t>(1, localNnz) * sizeof(index_t)),
   306	              "cudaMalloc(columns)", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   307	    cudaCheck(cudaMalloc(&dRows, (static_cast<size_t>(localRows) + 1) * sizeof(index_t)),
   308	              "cudaMalloc(rows)", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   309	    cudaCheck(cudaMalloc(&dVec, static_cast<size_t>(numRows) * sizeof(double)),
   310	              "cudaMalloc(vector)", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   311	    cudaCheck(cudaMalloc(&dOut, std::max<size_t>(1, localRows) * sizeof(double)),
   312	              "cudaMalloc(output)", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   313	    if (localNnz != 0) {
   314	        cudaCheck(cudaMemcpy(dVal, localVal.data(), localNnz * sizeof(double),
   315	                             cudaMemcpyHostToDevice), "copy values", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   316	        cudaCheck(cudaMemcpy(dCols, localCols.data(), localNnz * sizeof(index_t),
   317	                             cudaMemcpyHostToDevice), "copy columns", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   318	    }
   319	    cudaCheck(cudaMemcpy(dRows, localRowOffsets.data(), (localRows + 1) * sizeof(index_t),
   320	                         cudaMemcpyHostToDevice), "copy row offsets", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   321	    cudaCheck(cudaMemcpy(dVec, hVec.data(), numRows * sizeof(double), cudaMemcpyHostToDevice),
   322	              "copy dense vector", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   323	
   324	    // Host staging buffers are no longer needed during the benchmark. Keep the
   325	    // complete root-side matrix only when CPU validation was requested.
   326	    std::vector<double>().swap(localVal);
   327	    std::vector<index_t>().swap(localCols);
   328	    std::vector<index_t>().swap(localRowOffsets);
   329	    if (rank != 0) {
   330	        std::vector<double>().swap(hVec);
   331	    } else if (!validate) {
   332	        std::vector<double>().swap(hVal);
   333	        std::vector<index_t>().swap(hCols);
   334	        std::vector<index_t>().swap(hRows);
   335	    }
   336	
   337	    const int warpsPerBlock = CUDA_BLOCK_SIZE / WARP_SIZE;
   338	    const int blocks = (static_cast<int>(localRows) + warpsPerBlock - 1) / warpsPerBlock;
   339	    if (localRows != 0) {
   340	        // Untimed warm-up removes lazy context and instruction-cache costs.
   341	        spmvCsrWarpKernel<<<blocks, CUDA_BLOCK_SIZE>>>(dVal, dCols, dRows, dVec, localRows, dOut);
   342	        cudaCheck(cudaDeviceSynchronize(), "warm-up SpMV", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   343	    }
   344	
   345	    if (rank == 0) std::printf("Computing SpMV...\n");
   346	    MPI_Barrier(MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   347	    const double start = MPI_Wtime();
   348	    for (index_t iter = 0; iter < iterations; ++iter) {
   349	        if (localRows != 0) {
   350	            spmvCsrWarpKernel<<<blocks, CUDA_BLOCK_SIZE>>>(dVal, dCols, dRows, dVec,
   351	                                                           localRows, dOut);
   352	        }
   353	    }
   354	    cudaCheck(cudaGetLastError(), "SpMV kernel launch", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   355	    cudaCheck(cudaDeviceSynchronize(), "timed SpMV", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   356	    const double localSeconds = MPI_Wtime() - start;
   357	    double elapsedSeconds = 0.0;
   358	    MPI_Reduce(&localSeconds, &elapsedSeconds, 1, MPI_DOUBLE, MPI_MAX, 0, MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   359	
   360	    const bool needOutput = validate || printResults;
   361	    std::vector<double> localOut(needOutput ? localRows : 0);
   362	    if (needOutput && localRows != 0) {
   363	        cudaCheck(cudaMemcpy(localOut.data(), dOut, localRows * sizeof(double),
   364	                             cudaMemcpyDeviceToHost), "copy output", MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   365	    }
   366	    if (needOutput) {
   367	        MPI_Gatherv(localOut.data(), static_cast<int>(localRows), MPI_DOUBLE,
   368	                    rank == 0 ? hOut.data() : nullptr, rowCounts.data(), rowDispls.data(),
   369	                    MPI_DOUBLE, 0, MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   370	    }
   371	
   372	    cudaFree(dOut);
   373	    cudaFree(dVec);
   374	    cudaFree(dRows);
   375	    cudaFree(dCols);
   376	    cudaFree(dVal);
   377	
   378	    int exitCode = EXIT_SUCCESS;
   379	    if (rank == 0) {
   380	        const double milliseconds = elapsedSeconds * 1000.0;
   381	        const double gflops = (2.0 * nItems * iterations) / elapsedSeconds / 1e9;
   382	        std::printf("Computation time: %.3f ms\n", milliseconds);
   383	        std::printf("Average time per iteration: %.3f ms\n", milliseconds / iterations);
   384	        std::printf("Performance: %.3f GFLOPS\n", gflops);
   385	
   386	        if (printResults) {
   387	            print_results(hOut, "OutputVector");
   388	        }
   389	        if (validate) {
   390	            std::printf("Computing reference solution and validating result...\n");
   391	            std::vector<double> reference(numRows);
   392	            spmvCpu(hVal.data(), hCols.data(), hRows.data(), hVec.data(), numRows,
   393	                    reference.data());
   394	            if (verifyResults(reference.data(), hOut.data(), numRows)) {
   395	                std::printf("Validation: PASSED\n");
   396	            } else {
   397	                std::printf("Validation: FAILED\n");
   398	                exitCode = EXIT_FAILURE;
   399	            }
   400	        }
   401	    }
   402	
   403	    MPI_Bcast(&exitCode, 1, MPI_INT, 0, MPI_COMM_WORLD);
   404	    MPI_Finalize();
   405	    return exitCode;
   406	}

+static void cudaCheck(cudaError_t status, const char* operation, MPI_Comm comm) {
+    if (status != cudaSuccess) {
+        int rank = -1;
+        MPI_Comm_rank(comm, &rank);
+        std::fprintf(stderr, "Rank %d: CUDA error in %s: %s\n", rank, operation,
+                     cudaGetErrorString(status));
+        MPI_Abort(comm, EXIT_FAILURE);
+    }

+
 void fill(double* A, const index_t n, const double maxVal) {
     for (index_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
         A[i] = maxVal * (rand() / (static_cast<double>(RAND_MAX) + 1.0));
     }
 }

+void initRandomMatrix(index_t* cols, index_t* rowDelimiters, const index_t n,

     index_t nnzAssigned = 0;

-

     srand(8675309);

         for (index_t j = 0; j < dim; ++j) {

+            const uint64_t linear = static_cast<uint64_t>(i) * dim + j;

                 fillRemaining = true;

+
+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
     }
 }

codex
Implemented the hybrid MPI + OpenMP + CUDA SpMV benchmark while preserving the `spmv` executable name.

Key changes:

- MPI distributes contiguous CSR row partitions across ranks.
- Local MPI ranks automatically select available CUDA devices.
- CUDA warp-per-row kernel performs SpMV without atomics.
- OpenMP accelerates host preprocessing, reference computation, and validation.
- Timing uses the slowest MPI rank and excludes setup, transfers, warm-up, and result gathering.
- Native GPU architecture and optimized CUDA compilation enabled.
- Host staging memory is released after GPU upload.
- Only [spmv.cpp](/home/llmtest/evals/20260805-120633/spmv_gpt-5.6-sol-medium_hybrid_r4/spmv/spmv.cpp) and [CMakeLists.txt](/home/llmtest/evals/20260805-120633/spmv_gpt-5.6-sol-medium_hybrid_r4/spmv/CMakeLists.txt) were changed.

Validation passed with 1, 2, and 4 MPI ranks, including uneven and zero-row rank partitions.

Build and run:

```bash
cmake -S spmv -B spmv/build -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release
cmake --build spmv/build -j
OMP_NUM_THREADS=4 mpirun -np 4 ./spmv/build/spmv -n 4096 -s 10 -i 100 -v
```

For cross-compiling to specific cluster GPUs, supply `-DCMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=80` or the appropriate architecture list.

+void spmvCpu(const double* val, const index_t* cols, const index_t* rows,

+#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)

+
+// A warp owns a row. Threads access consecutive CSR entries and then reduce in
+// registers, avoiding atomics and intermediate storage. This is effective for
+// both short and moderately dense rows and is insensitive to row-length skew.
+__global__ void spmvCsrWarpKernel(const double* __restrict__ val,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ cols,
+                                  const index_t* __restrict__ rows,
+                                  const double* __restrict__ vec,
+                                  index_t localRows,
+                                  double* __restrict__ out) {
+    const int globalThread = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
+    const index_t row = globalThread / WARP_SIZE;
+    const int lane = threadIdx.x & (WARP_SIZE - 1);
+    if (row >= localRows) {
+        return;
+    }
+
+    double sum = 0.0;
+    for (index_t j = rows[row] + lane; j < rows[row + 1]; j += WARP_SIZE) {
+        sum += __ldg(val + j) * __ldg(vec + cols[j]);
+    }
+    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
+        sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffffu, sum, offset);
+    }
+    if (lane == 0) {
+        out[row] = sum;
     }
 }

Implemented the hybrid MPI + OpenMP + CUDA SpMV benchmark while preserving the `spmv` executable name.

Key changes:

- MPI distributes contiguous CSR row partitions across ranks.
- Local MPI ranks automatically select available CUDA devices.
- CUDA warp-per-row kernel performs SpMV without atomics.
- OpenMP accelerates host preprocessing, reference computation, and validation.
- Timing uses the slowest MPI rank and excludes setup, transfers, warm-up, and result gathering.
- Native GPU architecture and optimized CUDA compilation enabled.
- Host staging memory is released after GPU upload.
- Only [spmv.cpp](/home/llmtest/evals/20260805-120633/spmv_gpt-5.6-sol-medium_hybrid_r4/spmv/spmv.cpp) and [CMakeLists.txt](/home/llmtest/evals/20260805-120633/spmv_gpt-5.6-sol-medium_hybrid_r4/spmv/CMakeLists.txt) were changed.

Validation passed with 1, 2, and 4 MPI ranks, including uneven and zero-row rank partitions.

Build and run:

```bash
cmake -S spmv -B spmv/build -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release
cmake --build spmv/build -j
OMP_NUM_THREADS=4 mpirun -np 4 ./spmv/build/spmv -n 4096 -s 10 -i 100 -v
```

For cross-compiling to specific cluster GPUs, supply `-DCMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=80` or the appropriate architecture list.
